∫_0^α [1+a sin(2πx)][1+a sin(2π(α-x))] dx.
= α + a I1 + a I2 + a^2 I22.
I1=∫_0^α sin(2πx) dx = (1-cos2πα)/(2π).
I2=∫_0^α sin(2π(α-x)) dx = ∫_{-α}^0? Let u=α-x, x=0→α, u=α→0: ∫_α^0 -sin(2πu)du = ∫_0^α sin(2πu)du = I1. So 2a I1.
I22=∫_0^α sin(2πx) sin(2π(α-x)) dx.
Use product: sinA sinB = 1/2[cos(A-B)-cos(A+B)] =1/2[cos(2π(x - α + x)) - cos(2π(α))] =1/2[cos(4πx - 2πα) - cos(2πα)].
Integrate 0 to α: 1/2[ (sin(4πα - 2πα)-sin(-2πα))/(4π) - α cos2πα ] =1/2[ (sin(2πα)+sin(2πα))/(4π) - α cos2πα ] = sin(2πα)/(2π) - (α/2)cos(2πα).

### FFT quick objective and gradient helpers
Top-level functions:
- `next_power2(m)` perhaps.
- `convolution_c(p, fft_size=None)`: returns real c length 2n-1 with sum 1 if p normalized. Use FFT. Need avoid O(n) memory? n maybe up to 8192, L 16384, fine.
Implementation:
```
def fast_convolution(p):
    n=len(p)
    L=1
    while L < 2*n-1: L <<=1
    F=np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    C=np.fft.irfft(F*F, L)[:2*n-1]
    # remove tiny negative
    return C
```
For gradient, need weighted cross-correlation h with p. h length M=2n-1 (weights). Use rfft:
```
def cross_corr_hp(h, p, L): # corr[i]=sum_j h[i+j] p[j]
    Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,L); Fh=np.fft.rfft(h,L)
    corr=np.fft.irfft(Fh * np.conj(Fp), L) # length L
    return corr[:len(p)]
```
Check indexing: Fh*conj(Fp) corresponds correlation with h? Let's test small conceptual. Circular correlation (h ⋆ p)[m]=∑_n h[(m+n) mod L] conj(p[n]). In frequency: ifft(fft(h)*conj(fft(p))). Yes. For m=0..n-1, desired ∑_{j=0}^{n-1} h[m+j] p[j]. If L > m+n-1 (2n-2), no wrap. Need L >= 2n-1? m+j max n-1+n-1=2n-2, length 2n-1. yes.

Grad of G=∑ w_k c_k (w sum 1): c_k = ∑_i p_i p_{k-i} over valid. ∂c_k/∂p_m = 2 p_{k-m} if 0≤k-m<n, except if k even and m=k/2? The formula 2 p_{k-m} yields for m=k/2 gives 2p_m, correct derivative of p_m^2? Wait ordered sum includes term i=m,j=m only once, derivative 2p_m; other pairs if k-m=m? same. If k-m valid, 2 p_{k-m}. But is `corr` = ∑_{j} h[m+j] p[j]. Put i=m, k=m+j. Then ∑_k h_k 2p_{k-m}=2∑_j h[m+j] p[j]. Yes. Good.

So grad = 2 cross_corr_h(p).

Objective value max c = max C. Need weight h. Use stable softmax:
```
def softmax_weights(C, beta):
    s=C - C.max()
    e=np.exp(beta*s)
    # sum maybe 1; no need normalize? For logsumexp, weights normalized.
    return e/e.sum()
```
But if beta large, exp may underflow others okay. Need if C max repeated. To focus max, beta maybe 20*n? C scale ~1/n, beta*C spread maybe? If C max ~1/n, beta=10*n gives exp(c*n*10) max ~exp(10) not too big; differences among coefficients maybe 0.5/n, beta*diff ~5. Good. But if n=512, beta=5120; c*beta~5. To approximate max, beta large: maybe 100*n (max exp 100) okay. Use beta = max(20.0, 50.0*n)? For n=1024, c*beta ~102, exp 1e44 okay. Could be overflows if n=8192 beta=819200, c~0.0001, product 82, okay. Set beta = 100*n or `1000 / max(mean diff, eps)`. Simpler: beta = float(100) * n. If c max ~1/n, beta*max~100, exp underflow but stable; exp(0) max, exp(negative) small. Good.

### Linearized minimax approach
At p, let c = p*p. We want reduce max. For any p', linearized diagonal L_k(p') = c_k + 2∑ p_{k-i}(p'_i - p_i). The high diagonal directions. If we solve:
minimize t
s.t. L_k(p') ≤ t for all k (2n-1 constraints)
     p'_i ≥0, sum p'=1.
This is LP. It approximates making all linearized diagonals ≤t. Since actual p' p' may have second-order positive. But if step small, okay.
We can add trust region? Or simply solve for p' and mix: p_new = (1-α)p + α p_LP. Since p_LP maybe large; mixing controls step. Since objective homogeneous and we normalize. α maybe 1? The LP is exactly previous SOTA partly? They maximize sum b with f*g ≤M, not exactly. But similar.

Note c_k = ∑ p_i p_{k-i} ordered. 2∑ a p = 2c_k. So L_k(p') = c_k + 2∑ a p' - 2c_k = 2∑ a p' - c_k. At p'=p, 2c-c=c. Good. So constraint: 2∑ a_i p'_i ≤ t + c_k. Wait L≤t => 2∑ a p' - c_k ≤ t => 2∑a p' ≤ t + c_k. Coefficients A_k,i=2 p_{k-i}; b_k=t + c_k? In standard Ax≤b with variable t: 2a_i p_i - t ≤ c_k. So constants b=c_k. Nice! ### Implementation plan
Need final code robust within 400s. I'd implement multi-stage search:
- Set budget 400 seconds, reserve 5s final eval/return.
- Use local quick objective with FFT.
- Start with a few initial sequences of several lengths (128, 256, 512, 1024? maybe 2048). For each, compute score. Keep best.
- Improvement methods:
  1. **Linearized LP steps**: for selected sequence length (maybe 512/1024) iterate LP direction with mixing until time. This likely best.
  2. **Multiplicative gradient**: for larger length (2048/4096) to further reduce.
  3. **Block/perturbation local search** as final polish? maybe not needed.
- Need manage CPU/time.

Can `scipy.optimize.linprog` be called many times? Need test budget mentally. For n=512, LP constraints 1023, vars 513. HiGHS maybe ~0.1s? Unknown. Could do <100s. For n=1024, maybe 1s each. We can dynamic: try LP for n=256 and 512. Start at 256 and upsample to 512 if improved.

Fast convolution stable:
```
def fft_convolution(p):
    n = p.size
    L = 1
    while L < 2*n -1: L <<=1
    F = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    C = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)
    C = C[:2*n-1]
    # due to tiny imaginary discarded; if any negative small, clip? max unaffected if small pos/neg. To ensure no negative max? If all negative tiny impossible if true positive, but due to round. Use np.maximum(C,0) for max maybe.
    return C
```
For gradient using weights h same length M:
```
def influence_from_weights(p, h):
   # returns v_i = sum_{j=0}^{n-1} h[i+j] p_j
   n=p.size; L=... >= 2n-1
   Fh=np.fft.rfft(h,L); Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,L)
   v=np.fft.irfft(Fh*np.conj(Fp), L)[:n]
   return v
```

corr[m] = ∑_{j=0}^{3} h[(m+j) mod4] p[j]. m=0: h0p0+h1p1+h2? p2=0,h3=0 good. m=1: h1p0+h2p1+h0 p2=0 +h3 p3=0. good.

### Multiplicative / smooth gradient for larger n

```
for it in range(iters):
   c=conv(p); current=max; if time break

   vscale=np.max(np.abs(v))

```

```
def clean_sequence(p):
```

```
def quick_score(p):
   p=clean_sequence(p)
   n=p.size
   if n<1: return np.inf
   c=fft_convolution(p)
   if c.size==0: return np.inf
   maxc=float(np.max(c))
   if maxc<0 or not finite: return np.inf
   return float(2*n*maxc)
```

### Optimization routines
1. `multiplicative_refine(p, deadline, rng, beta_start, beta_end, eta_start, eta_end?)`:
Run until deadline, return best within. Use quick score. Use annealing: beta maybe from 100*n to 10*n? T=1/? Maybe use `beta = 50.0 * n` and update. `eta` maybe 0.2 to 1.0. Let p normalized. Loop:
```
best_p=p.copy(); best_s=quick_score(p)
n=p.size
for it in range(...):
  if time>deadline break
  c=fft_convolution(p)
  m=c.max()
  h=softmax_weights(c, beta)
  v=influence(p,h)
  v = v - v.mean()
  # To avoid huge at zeros: scale by n? 
  vscale = np.max(np.abs(v))
  if vscale <= 1e-15: break
  # Adaptive eta: reduce if p has zeros at high v? maybe.
  change = np.exp(-eta * v / vscale)
  # If any p zero and change<1? Multiplicative preserves zero. If need reintroduce mass where v low, zeros remain. Add small floor: p = (1-eps)*p + eps/n; restore entropy.
  p = p * change
  p = np.maximum(p,0)
  # Add small noise/floor to escape
  if rng.random()<0.1:
      p = p + 1e-8 * rng.random(p.size)
```

2. `lp_refine(p, deadline, max_lp_iters)`:
```
best_p=p; best_score=quick_score(p)
for it in range(max_lp_iters):
  if time>deadline: break
  c=fft_convolution(best_p)
  score=2n*max
  q=solve_lp_direction(best_p, c)
  if q is None: break
  # line search mixing
  improved=False
  for alpha in [1.0, 0.6, 0.35, 0.2, 0.1, 0.05, 0.02]:
      trial = (1-alpha)*best_p + alpha*q
      trial = clean_sequence(trial)
      s=quick_score(trial)
      if s < best_score*(1-1e-12):
          best_p=trial; best_score=s; improved=True; break
  if not improved:
       # try perturb q or p with small random, or break if no improvement for X
       # Could use q even if not immediate improvement with tiny step? Maybe not.
       stagnation +=1; if >3 break
```
But if LP q same each time due to no improvement, break. We can also perturb p before LP (mix with uniform 10%).

`lp_direction(p, c)`:
```
n=p.size; m=2*n-1
A=np.empty((m,n), dtype=float)
# Build columns: for i in range(n): A[i:i+n, i] = 2*p
for i in range(n): A[i:i+n,i] = 2.0*p
A_t = np.empty((m,n+1)); A_t[:,:n]=A; A_t[:,n]=-1.0
A_eq = np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1.0
b_eq=np.array([1.0])
b=c.astype(float)
obj=np.zeros(n+1); obj[n]=1.0
res=linprog(obj, A_ub=A_t, b_ub=b, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=[(0,None)]*n+[(None,None)], method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
if res.status==0: return clean_sequence(res.x[:n])
else: return None
```

Then no separate. Good.

`softmax_weights(c,beta)`:
```
def softmax_weights(c, beta):
   m=np.max(c)
   z=beta*(c-m)
   e=np.exp(np.clip(z, -50, 50)) # avoid over/under? z<=0; clip lower -50.
   s=e.sum()
   if s<=0: w=np.zeros_like(c); w[argmax]=1
   else w=e/s
   return w
```

`fourier_shape(n, params)`:
```
M = len(params)//2
x=np.arange(n)/ (n-1) if n>1 else [0]
q=np.ones(n)
for m in range(1,M+1):
   amp=params[2*m-2]; phase=params[2*m-1]
   q += amp * np.cos(2*np.pi*m*x + phase)
q=np.maximum(q, 1e-3)
q = q/q.sum()
return q
```
Using n/(n-1) for support [0,1]. Boundaries.

Could include a mode m=0? sum normalized, no.

### Refinement methods after DE
Use best sequence from DE. But note quick_score of sequence from DE normalizes; it is p sum1.

`gradient_refine` can be quite effective. Let's design it carefully.

```
def gradient_refine(p, deadline, rng, max_iters=None, beta_factor=100.0, step_values=(1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125)):
    p = clean_sequence(p)
    n=p.size
    best=p.copy(); best_s=quick_score(p)
    beta = beta_factor*n
    no_improve=0
    it=0
    while time<deadline:
       c=fft_convolution(p)
       h=softmax_weights(c,beta)
       v=influence(p,h)
       v = v - v.mean()
       vscale=np.max(np.abs(v))
       if vscale<1e-15: break
       base = v/vscale
       improved=False
       for step in step_values:
           # avoid negative logs; p>0 floor
           p_try = p * np.exp(-step * base)
           p_try = np.maximum(p_try, 0.0)
           p_try = p_try / p_try.sum()
           s=quick_score(p_try)
           if s < best_s - 1e-12:
               p=p_try; best=p_try; best_s=s; no_improve=0; improved=True; break
       if not improved:
           no_improve +=1
           # perturb: mix with random or local best or uniform
           r = rng.random()
           if r < 0.33:
               noise = rng.random(n)
               p_try = (1-0.02)*p + 0.02*(noise/noise.sum())
               s=quick_score(p_try)
               if s < best_s: best=p_try; best_s=s
               p=best
           elif r < 0.66:
               # mix with uniform small
               p_try = (1-0.1)*p + 0.1/n
               ...
           else:
               # reduce beta? Actually increase beta to sharper max
               beta *= 2.0
               # or accept a small random step even if worse if no_improve > some? Could use simulated annealing, but best must be tracked.
           if no_improve > 50: break
       # Occasionally increase floor?
       it+=1
    return best, best_s
```
This does many quick_score calls: up to 4 per iteration + perturbation. For n=1024 maybe okay but 200s. Could reduce: try only step=0.5 or 1.0 and on fail reduce. Use step schedule: start step=1.0; if fail, halve and reuse same v? Good.

But `np.exp(-step*base)` where base=(v-mean)/vscale. If v has high where bad, exp reduces. If base positive, reduce high; negative, increase low. Could make some p very small/large; clip nonnegative. Since sum normalize. If p has zeros, multiplying by exp finite keeps zero; to add to low v zeros, need floor/perturb. We'll add small uniform after many iterations:
```
if it % 10 ==0:
   p = 0.999*p + 0.001/n
```
But this may worsen; do if no_improve high.

Potential problem: `base` mean-subtracted, but multiplying p by exp(-base) also has net effect mean not preserved; normalization okay. The direction effectively decreases high v and increases low v multiplicatively. Good.

Can we use **two-sided base**? If v variation large at boundary high v, base negative in middle? Good.

Maybe use a **proposal with direct velocity** and line search; but multiplicative simpler.

We can also add **block perturb** local search for final: choose random block and multiply by exp(normal sigma), as previous. With FFT quick. It can escape. Use in last stage. Need some diversity.

### Fourier DE details and possible issues
The continuous q must be nonnegative. If amplitude bounds sum >1, floor may create zeros and high peaks. DE might set many amplitudes to reduce score at cost of zeros. We can include a penalty? We can adjust objective: if min q before floor < 0.2, add penalty maybe 1e-3? But final sequence can have zeros; objective alone may like zeros because reduces boundary peaks. But too sparse bad. Let quick_score handle.

### Let's think about optimizing with `scipy.optimize.minimize` on p directly using projected gradient and computing gradient of smooth max. Could possibly be faster and more directed than multiplicative. Let's design a robust projected gradient with backtracking and maybe momentum.

Parameters z (log p) easier. Objective F_β=logsumexp(β c). Use Adam. Since c scales p^2, log-param. We can implement gradient automatically via FFT. This could find minima from nonuniform starts. Newton not needed.

Implementation:
```
def adam_refine(p, deadline, rng, beta=50*n, lr=0.2):
   z = np.log(p + 1e-12)
   z -= z.mean()
   m = zeros; v = zeros
   t=0
   best=p
   while time:
      p = softmax_logz(z) # exp(z)/sum
      c=conv(p)
      G = lse(c,beta)
      h=softmax_weights(c,beta)
      grad_p = 2*influence(p,h)
      grad_z = p*grad_p - p*np.dot(p, grad_p)
      t+=1; ... Adam update z -= lr * mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+eps); z -= z.mean()
      # maybe keep z within [-10,10]
      z = np.clip(z, -10, 10); z -= z.mean()
      if G < best: update
```
But Adam step on z with lr 0.1 changes p relative ~10%; many steps. Could be good. However computing conv every step; similar to multiplicative. No line search but adaptive. Need maybe accept only best tracked; current z may worsen but Adam continues. We can also periodically reset best if good.

Maybe multiplicative with line search more reliable for monotone improvement. But Adam can explore.

Could combine: use multiplicative for monotone, Adam for escape.

#### clean_sequence
Need handle bool? Input from numpy. For final, list of floats.
```
def clean_sequence(x):
    try:
        a=np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return np.ones(64)
    if a.ndim !=1:
        a=a.ravel()
    if a.size ==0:
        return np.ones(64)
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s<=1e-12:
       a=np.ones(a.size if a.size>0 else 64)
    else:
       a=a/s
    if a.size > 0 and a.max()>1000.0: # after sum maybe no
       a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
       s=a.sum(); if s>0: a/=s
    return a
```
For quick, sum normalized to 1. For final, returning normalized sum=1; no cap issue.

#### fft_convolution
Need L next power of 2 >= 2n-1. Could use `1 << (2*n-2).bit_length()`? For n=1, 1.
```
def fft_convolution(p):
    n=p.size
    if n==0: return np.zeros(0)
    need=2*n-1
    L=1
    while L<need: L <<=1
    F=np.fft.rfft(p,L)
    C=np.fft.irfft(F*F,L)
    C=C[:need]
    # small imaginary? irfft real. Clip tiny negative? For max, set C = np.where(C<0 and >-1e-12,0,C) maybe.
    C[C < 0] = 0? If true negative due to structure? For nonnegative, true coefficients nonnegative; any negative is roundoff if small. But if large negative due to bug? no. Clip to 0 safe for max; if actual coefficients positive, negative round tiny. Use `C = np.maximum(C, 0.0)`.
    return C
```
This may hide negative if roundoff -1e-16; good.

quick_score:
```
def quick_score(p):
    p=clean_sequence(p)
    n=p.size
    if n==0: return np.inf
    c=fft_convolution(p)
    m=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if not math.isfinite(m) or m<0: return np.inf
    return float(2.0*n*m)
```
If p all zero clean makes ones.

#### softmax_weights
```
def softmax_weights(c, beta):
   c=np.asarray(c,float)
   if c.size==0: return c
   m=float(np.max(c))
   z=beta*(c-m)
   z=np.clip(z, -700.0, 0.0)
   e=np.exp(z)
   s=float(e.sum())
   if s<=0 or not math.isfinite(s):
      w=np.zeros_like(c); w[np.argmax(c)]=1.0; return w
   w=e/s
   return w
```
If beta too large maybe all underflow except max; okay.

#### influence
```
def influence(p,h):
    n=p.size
    need=2*n-1
    L=1
    while L < need: L <<=1 # enough as argued
    Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,L)
    Fh=np.fft.rfft(h,L)
    v=np.fft.irfft(Fh * np.conj(Fp), L)
    v=v[:n]
    v=np.where(v<0,0,v)? true v nonnegative; round small. Use np.maximum(v,0).
    return v
```
Need check L for correlation: We need L >= n + len(h)-1? h len=2n-1, p len=n; but for first n entries m+j max 2n-2, L >= 2n-1. yes.

#### resample
```
def resample_sequence(p,new_n):
   p=np.asarray(p,float)
   if p.size==new_n: return clean_sequence(p)
   if p.size==1: return clean_sequence(np.full(new_n,float(p[0])/new_n? Actually p sum1 so p[0]=1; to resample uniform, fill 1/new_n))
   x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,p.size)
   x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,new_n)
   q=np.interp(x_new,x_old,p)
   q=np.clip(q,0,1000)
   s=q.sum()
   if s<=0: q=np.ones(new_n)
   else: q/=s
   return q
```
If p has zeros at endpoints, interp keeps endpoints. Good.

#### fourier_sequence
```
def fourier_sequence(n, params, floor=1e-3):
    n=int(n)
    if n<=0: return np.ones(1)
    if n==1: return np.ones(1)
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    q=np.ones(n,float)
    m=(len(params)//2)
    for idx in range(m):
        amp=params[2*idx]
        ph=params[2*idx+1]
        k=idx+1
        q = q + amp * np.cos(2.0*math.pi*k*x + ph)
    # Or use np.cos(k*x*2pi+ph)
    q=np.maximum(q, floor)
    s=q.sum()
    if s<=0: q=np.ones(n)
    else: q=q/s
    return q
```
Using loop over m; for speed, could precompute x. For DE many calls, recomputing x each call expensive. Could store global? Top-level global `_DE_X` and `_DE_K`? But DE_N may change. Simpler: in `fourier_objective`, precompute? Can't closure. We can have global `_DE_X` and set in propose. `fourier_sequence` can take optional x? But DE objective only params. Let's have global `_DE_X`, `_DE_M`.


#### Block refine
Use as final polish with acceptance only if improves best.
```
def block_refine(p, deadline, rng):
  best=p.copy(); best_s=quick_score(p)
  n=p.size
  while time:
    cand=best.copy()
    width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//200), max(2,n//10)))
    start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
    cand[start:start+width]*=np.exp(rng.normal(0, sigma))
    # maybe symmetrize? no; asymmetry good
    cand=clean_sequence(cand)
    s=quick_score(cand)
    if s < best_s: best=cand; best_s=s
  return best,best_s
```
This can find small improvements. Use larger sigma 0.1-0.5.

#### LP refine from nonuniform
Code robust. Need catch exceptions and time.
```
def lp_refine(p, deadline, rng, max_n=1024):
   if p.size > max_n: return p, quick_score(p)
   best=p.copy(); best_s=quick_score(p)
   no_improve=0
   while time<deadline:
      c=fft_convolution(best)
      score=2n max
      if score<best_s: update? best already.
      q=lp_direction(best,c)
      if q is None: no_improve++; if >3 break
      improved=False
      for alpha in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.05):
         trial=(1-alpha)*best + alpha*q
         trial=clean_sequence(trial)
         s=quick_score(trial)
         if s < best_s - 1e-12:
             best=trial; best_s=s; improved=True; break
      if improved: no_improve=0
      else:
         no_improve+=1
         # perturb q with random or p
         if no_improve>3: break
   return best,best_s
```
But if p from uniform, no improve; p should be nonuniform from DE or random.


```
def build_lp_matrix(p,c):
   n=p.size; m=2*n-1
   A=np.empty((m,n+1), dtype=float)
   A[:,n]=-1.0
   A[:,:n]=0.0
   col = 2.0*p
   for i in range(n):
       A[i:i+n, i] = col
   return A, c
```
Actually for k row, coefficient for p_i is 2p_{k-i}; our column assignment A[i:i+n,i]=2p means row k=i+j gets p_j. Good.
For `i` from 0 to n-1, rows i..i+n-1. If i=0 rows0..n-1 p[0..]. If k=j, coefficient p_j? For k=0, i=0 j=0 p0; good. k=1: i=0 j=1 p1, i=1 j=0 p0; column0 row1 p0, column1 row1 p1; correct 2∑ p_{k-i}p_i. Good.

Let's define a top-level `random_fourier_sequence(n, rng, m, amp_bound=0.8)`:
```
def random_fourier_sequence(n,rng,m,amp_bound=0.8):
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    q=np.ones(n)
    for k in range(1,m+1):
       amp=float(rng.uniform(-amp_bound,amp_bound))
       ph=float(rng.uniform(0,2π))
       q += amp*np.cos(2π k x + ph)
    q=np.maximum(q,1e-3); q/=q.sum(); return q
```

Candidate list size maybe 50; sorting okay.

### More robust gradient step using current best c not candidate? 
`gradient_refine` computes c for p each iteration. If p not improved, c and h same; no improvement infinite loop unless perturb. We set max_no_improve.

Implement `gradient_refine` carefully:
```
def gradient_refine(p, deadline, rng, beta_factor=20.0, max_no_improve=60):
    p = clean_sequence(p)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0: return p, np.inf
    best = p.copy()
    best_s = quick_score(p)
    current_s = best_s
    beta = max(1.0, beta_factor * n)
    no_improve = 0
    no_perturb_success = 0
    steps = (0.8, 0.4, 0.2, 0.1, 0.05)
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = fft_convolution(p)
        # if current_s not accurate? quick_score uses max; use c max to update current?
        current_s2 = float(2*n*max(c.max(),0))
        if current_s2 < current_s - 1e-12:
            current_s = current_s2
        h = softmax_weights(c, beta)
        v = influence(p, h)
        v = v - float(v.mean())
        vs = float(np.max(np.abs(v)))
        improved = False
        if vs > 1e-14:
            base = v / vs
            for st in steps:
                # if st too large causing all zeros? p*exp(-st base) all positive unless base very negative and p zero.
                p_try = p * np.exp(-st * base)
                p_try = np.maximum(p_try, 0.0)
                s_try = float(p_try.sum())
                if s_try <= 1e-12: break
                p_try = p_try / s_try
                s = quick_score(p_try)
                if np.isfinite(s) and s < current_s - 1e-12:
                    p = p_try; current_s = s
                    if s < best_s:
                        best = p.copy(); best_s = s
                    no_improve = 0; improved = True
                    break
        if not improved:
            no_improve += 1
            # Adapt beta
            if no_improve % 10 == 0:
                beta *= 0.7
                if beta < 2.0*n: beta = 2.0*n
                # occasional perturb current even if not improving, but must not be too bad
                if no_improve > 20:
                    # try several perturbations
                    p_pert = None; s_pert = np.inf
                    for _ in range(5):
                        r = rng.random()
                        if r < 0.4:
                            noise = rng.random(n)
                            nt = 0.05 + 0.2*rng.random()
                            cand = (1-nt)*p + nt*(noise/noise.sum())
                        elif r < 0.7:
                            cand = 0.9*p + 0.1/n
                        else:
                            cand = p.copy()
                            width = int(rng.integers(max(1,n//100), max(1,n//10)))
                            start = int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
                            cand[start:start+width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.25)))
                        cand = clean_sequence(cand)
                        sc = quick_score(cand)
                        if sc < s_pert:
                            s_pert = sc; p_pert = cand
                    # accept perturbation if not much worse than best or better
                    if np.isfinite(s_pert) and (s_pert < best_s or (s_pert <= best_s*1.05 and no_improve > 30)):
                        p = p_pert; current_s = s_pert
                        best = p.copy(); best_s = current_s
                        no_improve = 10
                        no_perturb_success = 0
                    else:
                        no_perturb_success += 1
                        if no_perturb_success > 3:
                            break
        # floor to keep positive (very small)
        if no_improve % 50 == 0 and no_improve>0:
            p = 0.999*p + 0.001/n
            p = clean_sequence(p)
            current_s = quick_score(p)
            # do not update best if worse? p changed; okay.
    return best, best_s
```
This can be expensive: quick_score for each step (5 FFTs). 180s maybe thousands? Each FFT 2048 ~0.0005? 5*20000 maybe. Good.
But `quick_score` recomputes p normalization (clip) each time, adding O(n). Fine.
If p becomes zero at some entries, exp base doesn't introduce positive. Perturbations can.

Potential bug: current_s after perturbation accepted if worse than best? We set best to perturbation even if worse? In code above if accept condition includes `s_pert < best_s or (<=1.05*best and no_improve>30)`, then set best=pert; This might lower global best to a worse sequence, not desirable for final but for exploration. But we lose best score. Need keep `best` global best; `p` can be worse. If accept worse than best, do not update best. Modify:
```
if accept:
   p=p_pert; current_s=s_pert
   if s_pert < best_s: update best
   no_improve=...
```
Good.

Also floor `p = 0.999*p + ...` may worsen; don't update current_s? Set current_s to quick_score(p) but keep best. Good.

Maybe `current_s` can get much worse after perturbations, decreasing efficiency. Limit acceptance.

We can add a `current_trial_count` and if too many no improve reset p=best.

Simplify for reliability: Keep p=best always; no temporary. Then no stuck; but can miss. Maybe enough if good start. But if gradient in basin no improvement, random DE restart in main might help. Sticking simpler and safer. Let p=best only. In perturbation, only accept if improves best. If no improvement for max_no_improve break; main can start another candidate. This avoids wasting time and keeping best tracked.

I'll implement monotone monotone version for gradient/block, and use multi-candidate starts (DE) to escape. Simpler and robust. If a candidate stuck, try next. Need ensure main has time for next. Could run gradient on top 3 candidates with slices. This may be better than temporary.

So `gradient_refine` monotone:
```
p=clean(p); best=p; best_s=quick_score(p); current=p; current_s=best_s; no_improve=0
while time:
  c=...
  h... v...
  for step in steps:
    trial...
    if s < current_s - 1e-12: current=trial; current_s=s; if s<best_s: best=trial; best_s=s; no_improve=0; break
  if not improved:
    no_improve+=1
    if no_improve % 5 ==0:
       # perturb current but only if improves best? Could if improves current? If it doesn't improve current but current=best? current is best if monotone. If not, okay.
       cand random; if score < current_s: accept; no_improve=0
    if no_improve >= max_no_improve: break
    # adapt beta smaller
    if no_improve > max_no_improve//2: beta*=0.75
return best,best_s
```
Here current can be worse than best? No if only accept improvements from current, current always best (global). So random only if improves global. Good.

This may be too conservative but okay with multiple starts.

Set `max_no_improve` based on time; e.g., 200. But if no improve, break; main moves to next start.

### Potential performance issue: `quick_score` normalizes by sum every call using clean_sequence, which does nan_to_num and clip O(n). In fast iterations of gradient, we can skip full clean and just ensure nonnegative and sum. But simplicity okay for n=1024. However if many iterations, overhead maybe. We can create `quick_score_normalized(p_assumed_nonneg_sum1)`? But not necessary. Let's optimize a bit:
```
def normalized_score(p):
   n=p.size; s=p.sum(); if... c=fft_convolution(p/s); return 2n max
```
But `p` in gradient already normalized/clean. We can use `score_normalized(p)` that assumes finite nonnegative, only normalizes sum. Less overhead.
```
def score_normalized(p):
   p=np.asarray(p,np.float64)
   s=float(p.sum())
   if s<=0: return np.inf
   q=p/s
   c=fft_convolution(q)
   m=c.max(); return 2*p.size*m
```
In gradient, `p_try` normalized; we can use this. But quick_score used for arbitrary. Let's define both.

`fft_convolution` expects nonnegative; p_try positive.

```
def is_uniform_normalized(p, tol=1e-5):
    n=p.size
    if n==0: return True
    mean=1.0/n
    return float(np.max(np.abs(p-mean))) < tol
```
Since p normalized.

#### Helper: next_fft_size
```
def _fft_size_for(n):
    need=2*n-1 if n>0 else 1
    L=1
    while L<need: L <<=1
    return L
```

#### score_normalized
```
def score_normalized(p):
    p=np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.ndim !=1: p=p.ravel()
    if p.size==0: return np.inf
    q=np.where(p<0,0.0,p)
    # if tiny negative -1e-16, clip; if large negative maybe invalid but clean later.
    s=float(q.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
       return np.inf
    q=q/s
    c=fft_convolution(q)
    if c.size==0: return np.inf
    m=float(np.max(c))
    if not math.isfinite(m): return np.inf
    if m<0: m=0.0
    return float(2.0 * p.size * m)
```
For p with NaN? `np.where(p<0,0,p)` leaves NaN; sum NaN -> not finite. Good.

#### clean_sequence
```
def clean_sequence(x):
   try: a=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64)
   except: return np.ones(64)
   if a.ndim !=1: a=a.ravel()
   if a.size ==0: return np.ones(64)
   a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
   a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
   s=float(a.sum())
   if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
       a=np.ones(a.size)
   else:
       a=a/s
   # ensure values ≤1000 (after normalization ≤1 if a nonneg sum1 except n=0)
   return a
```
If input all zeros length 1000, clean returns ones (sum 1 each? Actually ones length 1000, then normalize? Code if sum zero: a=np.ones(a.size); but sum is 1000 not 1. Need normalize after? For all zero, set `a=np.ones(a.size); a=a/a.size`. Better:
```
if invalid or s<=: a=np.ones(a.size)/a.size
```
For normal, normalize. Good.
If input has large values sum >? normalization.

#### get_global_sequence
```
def get_global_sequence():
    try:
       h=globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    except Exception:
       return None
    if h is None: return None
    try: arr=np.asarray(h,dtype=np.float64)
    except: return None
    if arr.ndim !=1: arr=arr.ravel()
    if arr.size==0: return None
    arr=np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    if not math.isfinite(arr).all(): arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
    s=arr.sum()
    if s<=1e-12: return None
    arr=arr/s
    return arr
```
No lambda.

#### make_tilt_sequence
```
def make_tilt_sequence(n,a):
   n=int(n)
   if n<=0: return np.ones(1)
   x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   q=1.0 + a*(x-0.5)
   q=np.maximum(q,1e-3)
   q=q/q.sum()
   return q
```
Maybe also `make_shape`.

#### random_fourier_sequence
```
def random_fourier_sequence(n,rng,m=4,amp_bound=0.7):
   n=int(n)
   if n<=0: return np.ones(1)
   x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   q=np.ones(n)
   for k in range(1,m+1):
       amp=float(rng.uniform(-amp_bound,amp_bound))
       ph=float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0*math.pi))
       q = q + amp * np.cos(2.0*math.pi*k*x + ph)
   q=np.maximum(q,1e-3)
   s=q.sum()
   if s<=0: q=np.ones(n)
   else: q=q/s
   return q
```
Add maybe with chirp phase: ph random enough.

#### make_basic_candidates? Could be inside propose without helper. But helper saves.
```
def generate_initial_seqs(n,rng):
   out=[]
   out.append(np.ones(n)/n)
   for a in (-1.5,-1.0,-0.5,0.5,1.0,1.5): out.append(make_tilt_sequence(n,a))
   # quadratic shape
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   for b in (0.5,1.0):
      q=np.maximum(1.0 + b*(x-0.5)**2,1e-3); q/=q.sum(); out.append(q)
   for _ in range(3): out.append(random_fourier_sequence(n,rng,4,0.7))
   return out
```
List comprehension not? fine.

#### DE globals and functions
```
_DE_X = np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
_DE_COS = []
_DE_SIN = []
_DE_M = 0
_DE_FLOOR = 1e-3
_DE_DEADLINE = 0.0

def prepare_fourier_globals(n,m):
   global _DE_X, _DE_COS, _DE_SIN, _DE_M
   _DE_X=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,int(n))
   _DE_COS=[]; _DE_SIN=[]
   for k in range(1,int(m)+1):
       _DE_COS.append(np.cos(2.0*math.pi*k*_DE_X))
       _DE_SIN.append(np.sin(2.0*math.pi*k*_DE_X))
   _DE_M=int(m)
```

`fourier_sequence_from_globals`:
```
def fourier_sequence_from_globals(params):
   n=_DE_X.size
   if n==0: return np.ones(1)
   q=np.ones(n)
   for idx in range(_DE_M):
       amp=float(params[2*idx]); ph=float(params[2*idx+1])
       cosph=float(np.cos(ph)); sinph=float(np.sin(ph))
       q = q + amp * (cosph * _DE_COS[idx] - sinph * _DE_SIN[idx])
   q=np.maximum(q, _DE_FLOOR)
   s=q.sum()
   if s<=1e-12: q=np.ones(n)
   else: q=q/s
   return q
```
`fourier_objective`:
```
def fourier_objective(params):
   p=fourier_sequence_from_globals(params)
   s=score_normalized(p)
   if not math.isfinite(s): return 1e6
   return float(s)
```
`_de_callback`:
```
def _de_callback(x, convergence):
   return time.monotonic() >= _DE_DEADLINE
```
`run_fourier_de`:
```
def run_fourier_de(n,m,seed,time_limit):
   if time_limit <= 1.0 or n<=0:
      return random_fourier_sequence(n, np.random.default_rng(seed), m, 0.7)
   prepare_fourier_globals(n,m)
   global _DE_DEADLINE, _DE_FLOOR
   _DE_FLOOR=1e-3
   _DE_DEADLINE=time.monotonic()+time_limit
   bounds=[]
   for i in range(m):
      bounds.append((-0.9,0.9))
      bounds.append((0.0,2.0*math.pi))
   maxiter=120
   popsize=15
   try:
       res=differential_evolution(fourier_objective, bounds, maxiter=maxiter, popsize=popsize, tol=1e-4, atol=0.0, seed=int(seed), polish=False, callback=_de_callback, mutation=(0.5,1.0), recombination=0.7)
       params=res.x
   except Exception:
       # fallback random local search
       rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
       params=np.zeros(2*m)
       for i in range(m): params[2*i]=rng.uniform(-0.7,0.7); params[2*i+1]=rng.uniform(0,2π)
   p=fourier_sequence_from_globals(params)
   return p
```
If DE import fails? We import at top with try? Need if `differential_evolution` not defined. We can wrap:
```
try:
   from scipy.optimize import differential_evolution
except Exception:
   differential_evolution = None
```
In run check.

But task allows scipy, so okay.

#### lp_refine implementation details
Need detect if p uniform? If uniform, LP no improve; we can add small random perturbation before LP:
```
def lp_refine(p, deadline, rng, max_n=1024):
   p=clean_sequence(p)
   if p.size > max_n: return p, score_normalized(p)
   n=p.size
   # break symmetry if near uniform
   if is_uniform_normalized(p):
       p = make_tilt_sequence(n, float(rng.choice([0.8,1.0,1.2])? no rng.choice with list okay) )
   best=p.copy(); best_s=score_normalized(p)
   noimprove=0
   while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       c=fft_convolution(best)
       q=lp_direction(best,c)
       if q is None: noimprove+=1; if >3 break
       improved=False
       for alpha in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.05):
           trial=(1-alpha)*best + alpha*q
           trial=clean_sequence(trial)
           s=score_normalized(trial)
           if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_s - 1e-12:
               best=trial; best_s=s; improved=True; break
       if improved: noimprove=0
       else:
           noimprove+=1
           if noimprove>4: break
   return best,best_s
```
But if p from uniform and made tilt, okay. If p nonuniform but LP stuck, break.

Time: Building A each iteration expensive. But few. `lp_direction`:
```
def lp_direction(p,c):
   n=p.size
   if n<2 or n>4096: return None # safeguard
   m=2*n-1
   A=np.empty((m,n+1), dtype=np.float64)
   A[:,n] = -1.0
   # A[:,:n] initially zeros? We fill columns maybe need initialize all.
   A[:, :n] = 0.0
   col = 2.0 * p
   for i in range(n):
       A[i:i+n, i] = col
   # rows? Column i length n; A[i:i+n,i] length n. Good.
   b = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
   b = np.maximum(b, 0.0) # c nonnegative
   A_eq = np.ones((1,n+1), dtype=np.float64); A_eq[0,n]=0.0
   b_eq = np.array([1.0])
   obj=np.zeros(n+1); obj[n]=1.0
   bounds=[]
   for i in range(n): bounds.append((0.0,None))
   bounds.append((None,None))
   try:
      res=linprog(obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
   except Exception:
      return None
   if res.status == 0 and res.x is not None:
       x=res.x[:n].astype(np.float64)
       x=np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
       x=np.clip(x,0,1000)
       s=x.sum()
       if s>1e-12:
          return x/s
   return None
```
Memory: `A[:, :n]=0.0` then columns; okay.
Could use `A = np.zeros((m,n+1))`; then set. Simpler.
`b` length m. If c sum1. Good.

Potential bug: linprog with objective t but t unbounded below? We reasoned t≥0 due to constraints? If p has many zeros and c_k=0 for some k with coefficient zero, constraint -t ≤ 0 => t≥0. If all constraints with coefficient not zero maybe t could be negative if RHS c positive? Constraint 2a·x - t ≤ c; if t very negative, LHS large, no. So bounded.

Could add lower bound t=(0,None) to help:
`bounds.append((0.0, None))`. Yes, score coefficients nonnegative; t should nonnegative. Add. This prunes.
Then if optimum t=0 possible? Could be if p zero? Not. Good.

#### gradient_refine implementation monotone
Need choices step. Use `score_normalized`.
```
def gradient_refine(p, deadline, rng, beta_factor=20.0, max_no_improve=80):
    p=clean_sequence(p)
    best=p.copy()
    best_s=score_normalized(p)
    current=p.copy()
    current_s=best_s
    n=p.size
    beta=float(beta_factor*n)
    min_beta=max(1.0,2.0*n)
    no_improve=0
    floor_count=0
    steps=(0.8,0.4,0.2,0.1,0.05)
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        c=fft_convolution(current)
        # current_s maybe from eval; use c max for consistency
        cm=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
        cs=float(2.0*n*max(cm,0.0))
        if math.isfinite(cs): current_s=cs
        h=softmax_weights(c,beta)
        v=influence(current,h)
        v=v - float(np.mean(v))
        vs=float(np.max(np.abs(v)))
        improved=False
        if vs > 1e-14:
            base=v/vs
            # To avoid underflow exp on very negative? base in [-1,1], st<=0.8 -> exp ok
            for st in steps:
                p_try=current * np.exp(-st*base)
                p_try=np.where(p_try<0,0,p_try)
                ssum=float(p_try.sum())
                if ssum<=1e-12: continue
                p_try=p_try/ssum
                s=score_normalized(p_try)
                if math.isfinite(s) and s < current_s - 1e-12:
                    current=p_try; current_s=s
                    if s < best_s:
                        best=p_try.copy(); best_s=s
                    no_improve=0
                    improved=True
                    break
        if not improved:
            no_improve += 1
            # adapt beta: if no improve for a while, reduce to spread
            if no_improve % 10 == 0:
                beta = max(min_beta, beta*0.7)
            # try a few random perturbations; accept only if improves current (and best)
            if no_improve % 5 == 0:
                for _ in range(4):
                    r=rng.random()
                    if r < 0.4:
                       noise=rng.random(n); nt=0.02+0.2*rng.random()
                       cand=(1-nt)*current + nt*(noise/noise.sum())
                    elif r < 0.7:
                       cand=0.9*current + 0.1/n
                    else:
                       cand=current.copy()
                       width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//200), max(2,n//8))) if n>16 else 1
                       start=int(rng.integers(0, max(1,n-width+1)))
                       stop=min(n,start+width)
                       cand[start:stop] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.3)))
                    cand=clean_sequence(cand)
                    if is_uniform_normalized(cand): continue
                    sc=score_normalized(cand)
                    if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < current_s - 1e-12:
                       current=cand; current_s=sc
                       if sc < best_s:
                           best=cand.copy(); best_s=sc
                       no_improve=0
                       break
            if no_improve >= max_no_improve:
                break
            # floor positive
            if no_improve >= 40:
                current = 0.999*current + 0.001/n
                current = clean_sequence(current)
                current_s = score_normalized(current)
                # don't change best
                no_improve = 10
    return best,best_s
```
This can be slow because score_normalized for each trial recomputes FFT. But okay.
Possible issue: `current` can be worse than best? It updates only if sc<current_s; current starts best, so current is best unless floor worsened. Floor may make current worse; then subsequent improvements only relative to current; best remains. Could lead to lowering below best later. If current worsens a lot, hard. Maybe after floor, if current_s > best_s, set current=best? But floor intended escape. We can allow only if no_improve large and set current=best after some. Simpler: don't accept floor; instead if no_improve large break. Let's remove floor or make it reset to best:
```
if no_improve >= max_no_improve: break
```
No floor.
But if current has zeros, gradient may fail; perturbations can reintroduce if improve. Good.

Maybe accept random perturbation if it improves current, not best; current always best unless perturbation improves best. So it only improves. Good.

`is_uniform_normalized` call in each random trial maybe O(n); skip? Not necessary. We can remove uniform check to speed.

Use `n>16` for width. If n=1? gradient no.

`softmax_weights` for c with all zeros? c sum1 max positive. Good.

`influence` uses h length 2n-1; if n large 2048, L=4096, fine.

#### block_refine monotone
```
def block_refine(p, deadline, rng):
   p=clean_sequence(p); best=p.copy(); best_s=score_normalized(p)
   n=p.size
   if n==0: return best,best_s
   no=0
   while time<deadline:
      cand=best.copy()
      if n>1:
        width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//300), max(2,n//8)))
        start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
        cand[start:start+width]*=np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.25))
      cand=clean_sequence(cand)
      s=score_normalized(cand)
      if finite and s<best_s-1e-12:
         best=cand; best_s=s; no=0
      else: no+=1
      if no>100: break
   return best,best_s
```
Could also random scale entire block add/subtract. Multiplicative okay.

### Is `np.convolve` full of sequence with itself same as FFT linear convolution? Yes. `fft_convolution` returns linear convolution of p with p. `rfft` F*F not `F*conj(F)` because convolution, not correlation. For self-convolution with same sequence, F*F correct. Influence formula needed cross-correlation of h with p: we used Fh * conj(Fp), which is correlation (not convolution). Let's re-verify derivative/influence and correlation sign.

Derivative: grad_r = 2∑_k h_k p_{k-r} for valid 0≤k-r<n. Let v_r=∑_{j=0}^{n-1} h_{r+j} p_j. This is cross-correlation of h and p with lag r. In DFT, circular cross-correlation `ifft(FFT(h) * conj(FFT(p)))` gives `corr[m] = ∑_n h[(m+n) mod L] conj(p[n])` if using convention? Let's confirm with numpy: `np.correlate(h,p, mode='full')` maybe. Example h=[h0,h1,h2], p=[a,b]; L=4.
FFT product inverse at m=0: ∑_x h_x p_{(x-m) mod L}? Need derive: Circular correlation (h ⋆ p)[m] = ∑_n h[(m+n) mod N] conj(p[n]) corresponds to ifft(fft(h)*conj(fft(p)))? Let's test algebra: ifft(FFT(h)conj(FFT(p)))[m] = 1/N ∑k H_k conj(P_k) e^{2πi km/N} = 1/N∑k (∑i h_i e^{-2πik i/N})(∑j p_j e^{2πik j/N}) e^{2πik m/N}=∑ij h_i p_j 1/N∑k e^{2πik(m+j-i)/N}=∑ij h_i p_j δ_{i,m+j modN}=∑j h_{(m+j) modN} p_j. Yes matches desired. Good.
For m=1: h1 p0 + h2 p1 + h3? p2=0, good.
Thus influence correct.

```
def fourier_local_search(params, deadline, rng):
   global _DE_*? Use current globals and score_normalized on fourier_sequence_from_globals.
   best_params=params.copy(); best_p=...; best_s=score
   step_amp=0.1; step_phase=0.2

      improved=False
      # random move: pick idx, perturb amp or phase by delta
      for _ in range(20):
         idx=rng.integers(0,_DE_M*2)
         cand=params.copy()
         if idx%2==0: cand[idx]+=rng.normal(0,0.1) clip [-1,1]
         else: cand[idx]+=rng.normal(0,0.2) (wrap)
         p=fourier_sequence_from_globals(cand); s=score
         if s<best_s: accept
      if no improved after many: shrink step
   return best_p
```

#### `is_uniform_normalized`
```
def is_uniform_normalized(p, tol=1e-5):
    n=p.size
    if n==0: return True
    return float(np.max(np.abs(p - 1.0/n))) < tol
```

#### `add candidate` maybe top-level
```
def add_candidate(candidates, seq):
    p = clean_sequence(seq)
    s = score_normalized(p)
    if math.isfinite(s):
        candidates.append((s, p))
    return s
```
It returns score. In propose:


#### `select_search_seq`
```
def select_search_seq(candidates):
    best_p=None; best_s=np.inf
    for s,p in candidates:
       p=clean_sequence(p)
       if is_uniform_normalized(p): continue
       if s<best_s: best_s=s; best_p=p
    return best_p
```

#### `generate_initial_seqs`
Use `random_fourier_sequence` with rng. Ensure `n` not too small.
```
def generate_initial_seqs(n,rng):
    out=[]
    n=int(n)
    if n<4: n=4
    out.append(np.ones(n)/n)
    for a in (-1.5,-1.0,-0.5,0.5,1.0,1.5): out.append(make_tilt_sequence(n,a))
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    for b in (0.5,1.0):
       q=np.maximum(1.0 + b*(x-0.5)*(x-0.5),1e-3); q=q/q.sum(); out.append(q)
    # maybe sine/cos variants
    q=np.maximum(1.0 + 0.8*np.sin(2*math.pi*x),1e-3); q/=q.sum(); out.append(q)
    q=np.maximum(1.0 + 0.8*np.cos(2*math.pi*x),1e-3); q/=q.sum(); out.append(q)
    for _ in range(3): out.append(random_fourier_sequence(n,rng,4,0.7))
    return out
```
This creates many candidates (12) per n. For n=256 and 1024 ->24 FFTs, fine.

Maybe include `height_sequence_1` resampled to 512/1024 in initial? We add separately.

```
_DE_X = np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
_DE_COS = []
_DE_SIN = []
_DE_M = 0
_DE_FLOOR = 1e-3
_DE_DEADLINE = 0.0

def prepare_fourier_globals(n,m):
    global _DE_X, _DE_COS, _DE_SIN, _DE_M
    n=int(n); m=int(m)
    if n <=0: n=1
    _DE_X = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    _DE_COS = []
    _DE_SIN = []
    for k in range(1, m+1):
        ang = 2.0*math.pi*k*_DE_X
        _DE_COS.append(np.cos(ang))
        _DE_SIN.append(np.sin(ang))
    _DE_M = m
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing

    2 * n * max_k conv(a, a)[k] / (sum(a)^2).

The objective is scale invariant, so search runs on normalized p with sum(p)=1.
A candidate has score 2*n*max(p*p).  The main tools are:
  - fast FFT-based scoring and subgradient influence,
  - a low-dimensional Fourier/global optimizer to leave the uniform saddle,
  - linearized minimax LP steps as a local direction,
  - monotone multiplicative gradient / block perturbations for polishing.

The function propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=400) returns the best list
found before the deadline.
"""

import math
import time
import numpy as np

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    from scipy.optimize import differential_evolution
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
except Exception:
    linprog = None
    differential_evolution = None
    minimize = None


# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Global workspace for the Fourier objective (kept top-level, no closures).
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------

_DE_X = np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
_DE_COS = []
_DE_SIN = []
_DE_M = 0
_DE_FLOOR = 1e-3
_DE_DEADLINE = 0.0


def clean_sequence(x):
    """Convert to a finite non-negative normalized numpy array."""
    try:
        a = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64) / 64.0

    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64) / 64.0

    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)

    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)

    a = a / s
    return a


def fft_convolution(p):
    """Linear convolution p*p by zero-padded FFT. Input non-negative."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    need = 2 * n - 1
    L = 1
    while L < need:
        L <<= 1

    F = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    C = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)
    C = C[:need]

    # For non-negative inputs all true coefficients are non-negative;
    # tiny negatives are FFT roundoff.
    C = np.maximum(C, 0.0)
    return C


def score_normalized(p):
    """Fast score assuming p can be normalized to sum 1."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.ndim != 1:
        p = p.ravel()
    if p.size == 0:
        return np.inf

    q = np.where(p < 0.0, 0.0, p)
    s = float(np.sum(q))
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return np.inf

    q = q / s
    c = fft_convolution(q)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf

    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not math.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    if m < 0.0:
        m = 0.0

    return float(2.0 * p.size * m)


def add_candidate(candidates, seq):
    """Clean seq, compute fast score, append to candidate list."""
    p = clean_sequence(seq)
    sc = score_normalized(p)
    if math.isfinite(sc):
        candidates.append((sc, p))
    return sc, p


def softmax_weights(c, beta):
    """Stable normalized weights approximating the maximum coefficient."""
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    if c.size == 0:
        return c

    m = float(np.max(c))
    z = beta * (c - m)
    z = np.clip(z, -700.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(e))

    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros_like(c, dtype=np.float64)
        idx = int(np.argmax(c))
        w[idx] = 1.0
        return w

    return e / s


def influence(p, h):
    """
    v_i = sum_j h[i+j] p_j.
    This is (up to a factor 2) the derivative of a weighted sum of c_k
    with respect to p_i.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    need = 2 * n - 1
    L = 1
    while L < need:
        L <<= 1

    Fp = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    Fh = np.fft.rfft(h, L)
    v = np.fft.irfft(Fh * np.conj(Fp), L)
    v = v[:n]
    v = np.maximum(v, 0.0)
    return v


def is_uniform_normalized(p, tol=1e-5):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return True
    return float(np.max(np.abs(p - 1.0 / n))) < tol


def resample_sequence(p, new_n):
    """Resample a normalized sequence to another length by linear interpolation."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.ndim != 1:
        p = p.ravel()
    new_n = int(new_n)

    if p.size == 0 or new_n <= 0:
        return np.ones(max(1, new_n), dtype=np.float64) / max(1, new_n)

    if new_n == 1:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)

    if p.size == 1:
        return np.full(new_n, 1.0 / float(new_n), dtype=np.float64)

    if p.size == new_n:
        return clean_sequence(p)

    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, p.size, dtype=np.float64)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, new_n, dtype=np.float64)
    q = np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
    q = np.clip(q, 0.0, 1000.0)

    s = float(np.sum(q))
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return np.ones(new_n, dtype=np.float64) / new_n

    q = q / s
    return q


def get_global_sequence():
    """Recover height_sequence_1 if the environment provides it."""
    try:
        h = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    except Exception:
        return None

    if h is None:
        return None

    try:
        arr = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None

    if arr.ndim != 1:
        arr = arr.ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None

    arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)

    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return None

    arr = arr / s

    # Keep returned direct-evaluted sequences operational. Extremely long
    # sequences are too slow for np.convolve in the final evaluation.
    if arr.size > 20000:
        arr = resample_sequence(arr, 4096)

    return arr


def make_tilt_sequence(n, a):
    """Asymmetric linear ramp, useful to break the uniform saddle."""
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    q = 1.0 + a * (x - 0.5)
    q = np.maximum(q, 1e-3)
    s = float(np.sum(q))
    if s <= 1e-12:
        q = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        q = q / s
    return q


def random_fourier_sequence(n, rng, m=4, amp_bound=0.7):
    """Random positive Fourier shape."""
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    q = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    for k in range(1, int(m) + 1):
        amp = float(rng.uniform(-amp_bound, amp_bound))
        ph = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * math.pi))
        q = q + amp * np.cos(2.0 * math.pi * k * x + ph)

    q = np.maximum(q, 1e-3)
    s = float(np.sum(q))
    if s <= 1e-12:
        q = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        q = q / s
    return q


def generate_initial_seqs(n, rng):
    """Small pool of diverse heuristic starts for a fixed length."""
    n = int(n)
    if n < 4:
        n = 4

    out = []

    # Uniform baseline.
    out.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n))

    # Asymmetric tilts.
    for a in (-1.5, -1.0, -0.5, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5):
        out.append(make_tilt_sequence(n, a))

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)

    # Low-degree symmetric shapes.
    for b in (0.5, 1.0):
        q = np.maximum(1.0 + b * (x - 0.5) * (x - 0.5), 1e-3)
        q = q / float(np.sum(q))
        out.append(q)

    q = np.maximum(1.0 + 0.8 * np.sin(2.0 * math.pi * x), 1e-3)
    q = q / float(np.sum(q))
    out.append(q)

    q = np.maximum(1.0 + 0.8 * np.cos(2.0 * math.pi * x), 1e-3)
    q = q / float(np.sum(q))
    out.append(q)

    # Random Fourier shapes.
    for _ in range(3):
        out.append(random_fourier_sequence(n, rng, m=4, amp_bound=0.7))

    return out


def prepare_fourier_globals(n, m):
    """Precompute Fourier basis for DE objective."""
    global _DE_X, _DE_COS, _DE_SIN, _DE_M

    n = int(n)
    m = int(m)
    if n <= 0:
        n = 1
    if m <= 0:
        m = 1

    _DE_X = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    _DE_COS = []
    _DE_SIN = []

    for k in range(1, m + 1):
        ang = 2.0 * math.pi * k * _DE_X
        _DE_COS.append(np.cos(ang))
        _DE_SIN.append(np.sin(ang))

    _DE_M = m


def fourier_sequence_from_globals(params):
    """Build normalized positive sequence from current Fourier globals."""
    n = _DE_X.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

    params = np.asarray(params, dtype=np.float64)
    q = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    for idx in range(_DE_M):
        if 2 * idx + 1 >= params.size:
            break
        amp = float(params[2 * idx])
        ph = float(params[2 * idx + 1])
        cph = float(np.cos(ph))
        sph = float(np.sin(ph))
        q = q + amp * (cph * _DE_COS[idx] - sph * _DE_SIN[idx])

    q = np.maximum(q, _DE_FLOOR)
    s = float(np.sum(q))
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        q = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        q = q / s
    return q


def fourier_objective(params):
    """Objective used by differential evolution."""
    p = fourier_sequence_from_globals(params)
    sc = score_normalized(p)
    if not math.isfinite(sc):
        return 1.0e6
    return float(sc)


def _de_callback(x, convergence):
    """Stop DE when global deadline is hit."""
    return time.monotonic() >= _DE_DEADLINE


def run_fourier_de(n, m, seed, time_limit):
    """Run low-dimensional DE over Fourier amplitudes/phases."""
    global _DE_FLOOR, _DE_DEADLINE

    n = int(max(1, n))
    m = int(max(1, m))
    time_limit = float(max(0.0, time_limit))

    if time_limit <= 1.0:
        return random_fourier_sequence(n, np.random.default_rng(int(seed)), m=m, amp_bound=0.7)

    prepare_fourier_globals(n, m)
    _DE_FLOOR = 1e-3
    _DE_DEADLINE = time.monotonic() + time_limit

    params = np.zeros(2 * m, dtype=np.float64)
    best_fun = np.inf

    seed = int(seed) % (2**31 - 1)

    if differential_evolution is not None:
        bounds = []
        for _ in range(m):
            bounds.append((-0.9, 0.9))
            bounds.append((0.0, 2.0 * math.pi))

        try:
            res = differential_evolution(
                fourier_objective,
                bounds,
                maxiter=120,
                popsize=15,
                tol=1e-4,
                atol=0.0,
                seed=seed,
                polish=False,
                callback=_de_callback,
                mutation=(0.5, 1.0),
                recombination=0.7,
                strategy="best1bin",
            )
            if res is not None and getattr(res, "x", None) is not None:
                params = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
                best_fun = float(getattr(res, "fun", np.inf))
        except Exception:
            params = np.zeros(2 * m, dtype=np.float64)
            best_fun = np.inf

    # If DE did not run or failed, seed with random candidates and evaluate.
    if not math.isfinite(best_fun):
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
        best_p = None
        best_local = np.inf

        cat = 0
        while cat < 300 and time.monotonic() < _DE_DEADLINE:
            cand = np.zeros(2 * m, dtype=np.float64)
            for i in range(m):
                cand[2 * i] = float(rng.uniform(-0.8, 0.8))
                cand[2 * i + 1] = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * math.pi))

            p = fourier_sequence_from_globals(cand)
            sc = score_normalized(p)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_local:
                best_local = sc
                best_p = p.copy()
                params = cand
            cat += 1

        if best_p is not None:
            return best_p

    if minimize is not None and time.monotonic() < _DE_DEADLINE - 4.0:
        try:
            local = minimize(
                fourier_objective,
                params,
                method="Nelder-Mead",
                options={
                    "maxiter": 220,
                    "maxfev": 1200,
                    "fatol": 1e-8,
                    "xatol": 1e-6,
                },
            )
            if local is not None and getattr(local, "fun", np.inf) < best_fun:
                params = np.asarray(local.x, dtype=np.float64)
        except Exception:
            pass

    p = fourier_sequence_from_globals(params)
    return p


def lp_direction(p, c):
    """
    Solve a linearized minimax LP around current p.

    Linearized diagonal:
        L_k(q) = 2 * sum_i p[k-i] q_i - c_k
    constraints L_k(q) <= t, q_i >= 0, sum q = 1.
    """
    if linprog is None:
        return None

    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size

    if n < 2 or n > 4096:
        return None

    m = 2 * n - 1
    if c.size != m:
        return None

    A = np.zeros((m, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
    A[:, n] = -1.0

    col = 2.0 * p
    for i in range(n):
        A[i:i + n, i] = col

    b = np.clip(c, 0.0, None)

    A_eq = np.ones((1, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
    A_eq[0, n] = 0.0
    b_eq = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)

    obj = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    obj[n] = 1.0

    bounds = [(0.0, None)] * (n + 1)

    try:
        res = linprog(
            obj,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b,
            A_eq=A_eq,
            b_eq=b_eq,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
        )
    except Exception:
        return None

    if res is None or getattr(res, "status", 1) != 0:
        return None

    x = getattr(res, "x", None)
    if x is None:
        return None

    x = np.asarray(x[:n], dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.nan_to_num(x, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1000.0)

    s = float(np.sum(x))
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return None

    return x / s


def lp_refine(p, deadline, rng, max_n=512):
    """Local LP-directional local search with mixing/line search."""
    p = clean_sequence(p)
    n = p.size

    if n < 2 or n > max_n:
        return p, score_normalized(p)

    # The uniform point is a symmetric saddle; break symmetry for LP.
    if is_uniform_normalized(p):
        p = make_tilt_sequence(n, 1.0)

    best = p.copy()
    best_s = score_normalized(p)
    no_improve = 0
    alphas = (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.05)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = fft_convolution(best)
        q = lp_direction(best, c)

        if q is None:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 3:
                break

        improved = False
        for alpha in alphas:
            trial = (1.0 - alpha) * best + alpha * q
            trial = clean_sequence(trial)
            s = score_normalized(trial)

            if math.isfinite(s) and s < best_s - 1e-12:
                best = trial
                best_s = s
                improved = True
                break

        if improved:
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 4:
                break

    return best, best_s


def gradient_refine(p, deadline, rng, beta_factor=20.0, max_no_improve=90):
    """
    Monotone multiplicative subgradient local search.
    p stays non-negative and sum 1; the best sequence is tracked separately.
    """
    p = clean_sequence(p)
    n = p.size
    if n < 2:
        return p, score_normalized(p)

    best = p.copy()
    best_s = score_normalized(p)
    current = p.copy()
    current_s = best_s

    beta = max(1.0, beta_factor * float(n))
    min_beta = max(1.0, 2.0 * float(n))
    no_improve = 0
    steps = (0.8, 0.4, 0.2, 0.1, 0.05)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = fft_convolution(current)
        cm = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
        if math.isfinite(cm):
            current_s = float(2.0 * n * max(cm, 0.0))

        h = softmax_weights(c, beta)
        v = influence(current, h)
        v = v - float(np.mean(v))
        vs = float(np.max(np.abs(v)))

        improved = False
        if vs > 1e-14:
            base = v / vs
            for st in steps:
                p_try = current * np.exp(-st * base)
                p_try = np.where(p_try < 0.0, 0.0, p_try)
                ssum = float(np.sum(p_try))
                if not math.isfinite(ssum) or ssum <= 1e-12:
                    continue
                p_try = p_try / ssum

                s = score_normalized(p_try)
                if math.isfinite(s) and s < current_s - 1e-12:
                    current = p_try
                    current_s = s
                    if s < best_s:
                        best = p_try.copy()
                        best_s = s
                    no_improve = 0
                    improved = True
                    break

        if not improved:
            no_improve += 1

            if no_improve % 10 == 0:
                beta = max(min_beta, beta * 0.7)

            # Occasional diversification, accepted only if it improves current.
            if no_improve % 5 == 0:
                for _ in range(4):
                    r = float(rng.random())
                    if r < 0.4:
                        noise = rng.random(n)
                        nt = 0.02 + 0.20 * float(rng.random())
                        cand = (1.0 - nt) * current + nt * (noise / float(np.sum(noise)))
                    elif r < 0.7:
                        cand = 0.90 * current + 0.10 / float(n)
                    else:
                        if n > 8:
                            width = int(rng.integers(max(1, n // 200), max(2, n // 8)))
                            start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
                            cand = current.copy()
                            stop = min(n, start + width)
                            cand[start:stop] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.3)))
                        else:
                            continue

                    cand = clean_sequence(cand)
                    sc = score_normalized(cand)
                    if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < current_s - 1e-12:
                        current = cand
                        current_s = sc
                        if sc < best_s:
                            best = cand.copy()
                            best_s = sc
                        no_improve = 0
                        break

            if no_improve >= max_no_improve:
                break

    return best, best_s


def block_refine(p, deadline, rng):
    """Cheap multiplicative block-polish, useful after gradient/LP."""
    p = clean_sequence(p)
    n = p.size
    if n < 2:
        return p, score_normalized(p)

    best = p.copy()
    best_s = score_normalized(p)
    no_improve = 0
    alphas = (1.0,)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        cand = best.copy()

        if n > 8:
            width = int(rng.integers(max(1, n // 300), max(2, n // 8)))
            start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
            stop = min(n, start + width)
            cand[start:stop] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.25)))

        cand = clean_sequence(cand)
        s = score_normalized(cand)

        if math.isfinite(s) and s < best_s - 1e-12:
            best = cand
            best_s = s
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 200:
                break

    return best, best_s


def select_search_seq(candidates):
    """Select best non-uniform candidate for continued search."""
    best_p = None
    best_s = np.inf

    for sc, p in candidates:
        p = clean_sequence(p)
        if is_uniform_normalized(p):
            continue
        if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_s:
            best_s = sc
            best_p = p.copy()

    return best_p


def candidate_sort_key(item):
    return item[0]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=400, **kwargs):
    """Main time-bounded search; returns best sequence as a list of floats."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    t0 = time.monotonic()

    total = max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 3.0)
    if total < 5.0:
        p = np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64) / 512.0
        return [float(x) for x in p]

    main_deadline = t0 + total
    final_reserve = 8.0
    work_deadline = max(t0 + 1.0, main_deadline - final_reserve)

    candidates = []
    best_seq = np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64) / 512.0
    best_score = score_normalized(best_seq)
    candidates.append((best_score, best_seq.copy()))

    def local_update(seq):
        nonlocal best_score, best_seq
        sc, p = add_candidate(candidates, seq)
        if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = p.copy()
        return sc

    # Seed with supplied construction if available.
    gseq = get_global_sequence()
    if gseq is not None:
        local_update(gseq)

    # Quick diverse starts at two lengths.
    for n in (256, 1024):
        for p0 in generate_initial_seqs(n, rng):
            local_update(p0)
            if time.monotonic() >= work_deadline:
                break

    print("initial best quick score", float(best_score))

    # Global Fourier optimization to leave the uniform manifold.
    now = time.monotonic()
    de_b = min(45.0, max(5.0, (work_deadline - now) * 0.12))
    if de_b >= 5.0 and now + 2.0 < work_deadline:
        p_de = run_fourier_de(
            1024,
            4,
            int(rng.integers(0, 2**31 - 1)),
            min(de_b, work_deadline - now - 5.0),
        )
        sc_de = local_update(p_de)
        print("DE score", float(sc_de) if math.isfinite(sc_de) else np.inf)

        now = time.monotonic()
        if now + 10.0 < work_deadline:
            p_de2 = run_fourier_de(
                512,
                3,
                int(rng.integers(0, 2**31 - 1)),
                min(25.0, work_deadline - now - 5.0),
            )
            local_update(p_de2)

    # Ensure we have a non-uniform search seed.
    search_seq = select_search_seq(candidates)
    if search_seq is None:
        search_seq = make_tilt_sequence(1024, 1.0)
    if search_seq.size > 12000:
        search_seq = resample_sequence(search_seq, 4096)
    local_update(search_seq)

    # LP stage on short non-uniform starts.
    now = time.monotonic()
    lp_b = min(80.0, max(5.0, (work_deadline - now) * 0.20))
    if lp_b >= 5.0 and now + 5.0 < work_deadline:
        candidates.sort(key=candidate_sort_key)
        lp_starts = []
        cnt = 0

        for sc, p in candidates:
            pp = clean_sequence(p)
            if is_uniform_normalized(pp):
                continue
            if pp.size > 512:
                target = 512
            else:
                target = pp.size
            if target < 32 and pp.size > 1:
                target = max(32, target)
            if target > 512:
                target = 512
            lp_starts.append(resample_sequence(pp, target))
            cnt += 1
            if cnt >= 2:
                break

        if not lp_starts:
            lp_starts.append(resample_sequence(search_seq, 512))

        lp_deadline = min(work_deadline, time.monotonic() + lp_b)
        per = max(5.0, min(40.0, lp_b / 2.0))

        for p0 in lp_starts:
            now = time.monotonic()
            if now >= lp_deadline:
                break
            limit = min(lp_deadline, now + per)
            p_lp, s_lp = lp_refine(p0, limit, rng, max_n=512)
            if math.isfinite(s_lp):
                local_update(p_lp)
                for target in (1024, 2048):
                    if p_lp.size != target and s_lp < best_score + 0.5:
                        local_update(resample_sequence(p_lp, target))

    # Gradient stage on the strongest non-uniform starts.
    now = time.monotonic()
    grad_b = min(180.0, max(10.0, (work_deadline - now) * 0.45))
    if grad_b >= 5.0 and now + 5.0 < work_deadline:
        grad_deadline = min(work_deadline, now + grad_b)

        candidates.sort(key=candidate_sort_key)
        starts = []
        cnt = 0

        for sc, p in candidates:
            pp = clean_sequence(p)
            if is_uniform_normalized(pp):
                continue
            if pp.size > 12000:
                pp = resample_sequence(pp, 4096)
            starts.append(pp.copy())
            cnt += 1
            if cnt >= 3:
                break

        if not starts:
            starts.append(search_seq.copy())

        # Add one or two diverse starts if room remains.
        try:
            nstart = int(starts[0].size)
            if nstart < 256:
                nstart = 256
            if nstart > 2048:
                nstart = 2048
        except Exception:
            nstart = 1024

        if len(starts) < 4 and starts[0].size != nstart:
            starts.append(make_tilt_sequence(nstart, 1.0))
        if len(starts) < 4:
            starts.append(random_fourier_sequence(nstart, rng, m=4, amp_bound=0.7))

        starts = starts[:3]
        time_slice = max(5.0, (grad_deadline - time.monotonic()) / max(1, len(starts)))

        for idx, p0 in enumerate(starts):
            now = time.monotonic()
            if now >= grad_deadline:
                break
            if idx == len(starts) - 1:
                limit = grad_deadline
            else:
                limit = min(grad_deadline, now + time_slice)

            p_gr, s_gr = gradient_refine(p0, limit, rng, beta_factor=20.0)
            if math.isfinite(s_gr):
                local_update(p_gr)
                for target in (1024, 2048):
                    if p_gr.size != target:
                        local_update(resample_sequence(p_gr, target))

    # Final blocked polish.
    block_deadline = work_deadline
    now = time.monotonic()
    if now + 5.0 < block_deadline:
        candidates.sort(key=candidate_sort_key)
        search2 = select_search_seq(candidates)
        if search2 is None:
            search2 = best_seq

        if search2.size > 12000:
            search2 = resample_sequence(search2, 4096)

        remaining = max(0.0, block_deadline - now)
        per = max(5.0, min(60.0, remaining / 2.0))

        p_b1, s_b1 = block_refine(search2, min(block_deadline, time.monotonic() + per), rng)
        if math.isfinite(s_b1):
            local_update(p_b1)

        if best_seq is not None and time.monotonic() + 3.0 < block_deadline:
            p_b2 = best_seq.copy()
            if p_b2.size > 12000:
                p_b2 = resample_sequence(p_b2, 4096)
            p_b2, s_b2 = block_refine(p_b2, block_deadline, rng)
            if math.isfinite(s_b2):
                local_update(p_b2)

    # Final direct evaluation of a few best quick-score candidates.
    candidates.sort(key=candidate_sort_key)

    direct_best_seq = None
    direct_best_score = np.inf
    seen_lengths = []
    direct_count = 0

    for sc, p in candidates:
        if direct_count >= 4:
            break
        if time.monotonic() >= main_deadline - 2.0:
            break

        p = clean_sequence(p)
        n = p.size
        if n <= 0:
            continue

        if n in seen_lengths and n > 1000:
            continue
        if n > 10000 and direct_count > 0:
            continue

        seen_lengths.append(n)
        try:
            ds = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in p]))
        except Exception:
            ds = np.inf

        direct_count += 1

        if math.isfinite(ds) and ds < direct_best_score:
            direct_best_score = ds
            direct_best_seq = p.copy()
            print("direct candidate score", float(ds), "length", int(n))

    if direct_best_seq is not None:
        final = clean_sequence(direct_best_seq)
        final_score = direct_best_score
        print("selected direct score", float(final_score), "length", int(final.size))
    else:
        final = clean_sequence(best_seq)
        final_score = score_normalized(final)
        print("selected quick score", float(final_score), "length", int(final.size))

    # Keep final size manageable for the external evaluator.
    if final.size > 10000:
        final = resample_sequence(final, 8192)
        final_score = score_normalized(final)
        print("resampled final quick score", float(final_score), "length", int(final.size))

    final = np.nan_to_num(final, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    final = np.clip(final, 0.0, 1000.0)

    fs = float(np.sum(final))
    if not math.isfinite(fs) or fs <= 1e-12:
        final = np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64) / 512.0
    else:
        final = final / fs

    return [float(x) for x in final]
```
